<P style="margin:0pt" align="center"><font face="Times New Roman">F-7</font></P>
<HR color=#C0C0C0 noShade>
<P style="page-break-before:always; margin:0pt"><P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>
<font face="Times New Roman"><B>MEDISTEM LABORATORIES, INC.</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center><font face="Times New Roman"><B>
<a name="NOTES_TO_CONSOLIDATED_FINANCIAL_STATEMENTS_">NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS</a></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><B>Note 1: &nbsp;Background and Basis of Presentation</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company was organized December 5, 2001 (Date of Inception) under the laws of the State of Nevada, as SGC Holdings, Inc. &nbsp;On November 4, 2005, SGC Holdings, Inc. (the &quot;Company&quot;) filed with the Secretary of State of Nevada an amendment to its Articles of Incorporation to effect a corporate name change to &quot;Medistem Laboratories, Inc.&quot; and its OTC Bulletin Board trading symbol was changed to &quot;MDSM&quot;.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">On October 12, 2005 the Company entered into a Contribution Agreement with Neil Riordan, whereby Mr. Riordan transferred all rights, title and interest to certain intellectual property in exchange for 100,223,602 shares of the Company's common stock. &nbsp;The agreement provides the Company with proprietary, licensing, patent, marketing and other intellectual property rights related to the intellectual property. &nbsp;This transaction was accounted for as a reverse merger and the intangible assets were carried forward at their original capitalized costs.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company's primary business is the discovery, development, and commercialization of adult stem cell products that address serious medical conditions. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company was a development stage company until the fourth quarter of 2006, when it began deriving significant revenues from its planned principal operations.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B>Note 2: &nbsp;Going Concern and Operations</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The accompanying financial statements have been prepared assuming the Company will continue as a going concern. &nbsp;The Company has incurred losses and operational cash outflows since inception, and has a limited history of revenues. &nbsp;The future of the Company is dependent upon future profitable operations and the development of new business opportunities. &nbsp;Management may need to raise additional funds via a combination of equity and/or debt offerings. &nbsp;&nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">These conditions raise substantial doubt about the Company's ability to continue as a going concern. These financial statements do not include any adjustments that might arise from this uncertainty.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B>Note 3: &nbsp;Summary of Significant Accounting Policies</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Principles of Consolidation</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The accompanying consolidated financial statements include the accounts of the Company and any entities determined to be variable interest entities for which the Company is the primary beneficiary. &nbsp;All intercompany accounts and transactions have been eliminated.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">On February 23, 2006, the Company entered into a licensing agreement with Institute for Cellular Medicine (&quot;ICM&quot;), a Costa Rican corporation that is controlled by the Company's Chief Executive Officer. &nbsp;This agreement was subsequently amended on November 10, 2006 to clarify certain provisions with respect to the computation of royalties. &nbsp;Under the terms of this agreement, which was effective retroactively to October 12, 2005, Medistem granted a license regarding certain intellectual property and has agreed to fund all necessary operating expenses in exchange for the receipt of 85% of the pretax income generated from the use of the intellectual property. &nbsp;&nbsp;The Company was also required to fund the licensee during the duration of the agreement. &nbsp;This caused us to consolidate ICM with Medistem, as required by
Financial Accounting Standards Board (&quot;FASB&quot;) Interpretation No. 46,
&quot;Consolidation of Variable Interest Entities, an Interpretation of ARB No. 41&quot;
as amended December 2003 (&quot;FIN No. 46&quot;).</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Effective December 31, 2007, this license agreement was modified to (i) remove the funding obligation contained in the original license agreement; (ii) change the royalty rate to 20 percent of net revenues; and (iii) extend the term of the agreement from expiring in 2010 to perpetuity. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
</CODE>
<p style="margin: 0pt">&nbsp;</p>
<p style="margin: 0pt" align="center">F-8</p>
<CODE>
<HR color=#C0C0C0 noShade>
<P style="page-break-before:always; margin:0pt"><P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
&nbsp;<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>
<font face="Times New Roman"><B>MEDISTEM LABORATORIES, INC.</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center><font face="Times New Roman"><B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Prior to the license modification at December 31, 2007, the Company determined that ICM
met the definition of a variable interest entity (&quot;VIE&quot;) through its existing
capitalization and license agreement with the Company, and that the Company is
the primary beneficiary of this VIE, as both terms are defined in FIN No. 46. &nbsp;As required by FIN No. 46, ICM was consolidated in the accompanying financial statements for the period ending December 31, 2006. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Concurrent with the license modification, the Company reevaluated ICM and determined that, based on the terms of the revised license agreement, ICM no longer met the definition of a
VIE. &nbsp;This determination was based on qualitative factors including the following:</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:36pt; text-indent:-18pt">
<font face="Times New Roman">&#8226;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:36pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">ICM's equity investment at risk is sufficient to permit the entity to finance its activities without additional subordinated financial support provided by any parties, including the equity holders. &nbsp;This determination was based on historical and projected operating results;</FONT></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:36pt; text-indent:-18pt">
<font face="Times New Roman">&#8226;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:36pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">ICM's equity holders have the following essential characteristics of a controlling financial interest</FONT></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:72pt; text-indent:-18pt">
<font face="Times New Roman">o</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:72pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">The direct ability to make decisions about the entity's activities through voting rights </FONT></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:72pt; text-indent:-18pt">
<font face="Times New Roman">o</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:72pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">The obligation to absorb the expected losses of the entity</FONT></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:72pt; text-indent:-18pt">
<font face="Times New Roman">o</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:72pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">The right to receive the expected residual returns of the entity.</FONT></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:36pt; text-indent:-18pt">
<font face="Times New Roman">&#8226;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:36pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">ICM's equity holders have voting rights that are proportionate to their economic interest</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Because ICM is no longer considered a
VIE, it has been deconsolidated as of December 31, 2007; thus, the assets and liabilities of ICM are not reflected in the Company's Balance Sheet at December 31, 2007 . &nbsp;No historical periods have been restated. &nbsp;However, the statements of operations included herein include the financial results of ICM through December 31, 2007, the
&quot;trigger&quot; date of de-consolidation. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Fair Value of Financial Instruments</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company's financial instruments are cash and equivalents, short-term investments, royalties receivable, accounts payable and long-term amounts due from the Company's licensee. &nbsp;The recorded values of cash and equivalents, short-term investments, royalties receivable and accounts payable approximate their fair values based on their short-term nature. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company's long-term amounts due from ICM ($695,127 at December 31, 2007) is an interest-free loan whose terms were stipulated under the license agreement that was in effect from October 12, 2005 to December 31, 2007. &nbsp;&nbsp;Market interest rates could not be determined due to the unique nature of the loan. &nbsp;However, using an estimated market rate of 15% and an estimated payback period of two years, the fair value of the outstanding loan is $597,353. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Use of Estimates</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The preparation of financial &nbsp;statements in conformity &nbsp;with generally &nbsp;accepted accounting principles requires management to make estimates and assumptions that affect &nbsp;the &nbsp;reported &nbsp;amounts &nbsp;of assets &nbsp;and &nbsp;liabilities &nbsp;and &nbsp;disclosure &nbsp;of contingent &nbsp;assets and &nbsp;liabilities at the date of the financial &nbsp;statements and reported &nbsp;amounts of revenues and expenses during the reporting &nbsp;period. &nbsp;Actual results could differ from those estimates.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Cash and Equivalents</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company considers all highly liquid investments with maturities from date of purchase of three months or less to be cash equivalents. Cash and equivalents consist of cash on deposit with foreign and domestic banks and, at times, may exceed federally insured limits. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Short-Term Investments</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Short term investments consisted of a six-month certificate of deposit held by ICM.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align="center"><font face="Times New Roman">F-9</font></P>
<HR color=#C0C0C0 noShade>
<P style="page-break-before:always; margin:0pt"><P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>
<font face="Times New Roman"><B>MEDISTEM LABORATORIES, INC.</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center><font face="Times New Roman"><B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Property and Equipment</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Property and equipment are stated at cost, net of accumulated depreciation and amortization. Depreciation and amortization is computed using the straight-line method over the estimated useful lives of the respective assets, generally three to five years. Leasehold improvements are amortized on a straight-line basis over the shorter of the assets' useful lives or lease terms.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Intangible Assets</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company's intangible assets consist of pending patents and intellectual property related to the clinical application of adult stem cell treatments on a fee-for-service basis. &nbsp;The Company will begin amortizing these costs when the applicable patent is issued.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Long-lived Assets</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">FASB Statement of Financial Accounting Standards No. 144 &nbsp;<I>&quot;Accounting &nbsp;for the Impairment or Disposal of &nbsp;Long-Lived &nbsp;Assets&quot;</I> &nbsp;which &nbsp;requires that long-lived assets to be held and used be reviewed for impairment whenever events or changes in circumstances &nbsp;indicate &nbsp;that the &nbsp;carrying &nbsp;amount &nbsp;of an asset &nbsp;may not be recoverable.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company evaluates its long-lived assets for impairment annually or whenever changes in circumstances indicate that the carrying amount of the asset may not be recoverable. &nbsp;Recoverability of assets to be held and used is measured by a comparison of the carrying amount of an asset to future undiscounted cash flows expected to be generated by the asset. &nbsp;If assets are considered to be impaired, the impairment to be recognized is measured by the amount by which the carrying amounts exceed the fair values of the assets. &nbsp;Assets to be disposed of are reported at the lower of carrying values or fair values, less costs of disposal.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Revenue Recognition</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company recognizes license revenues when such revenues are earned in accordance with the relevant license agreement. &nbsp;The Company's licensee, ICM, which was consolidated until December 31, 2007, recognizes revenue when the underlying services are rendered. &nbsp;All intercompany revenues are eliminated in consolidation.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Net Loss per Share</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Net loss per share is calculated in accordance with Statement of Financial Accounting Standards (SFAS) No. 128, Earnings Per Share. Under the provisions of SFAS No. 128, basic net income per share is computed using the weighted average number of common shares outstanding during the period except that it does not include unvested restricted stock subject to cancellation. Diluted net income per share is computed using the weighted average number of common shares and, if dilutive, potential common shares outstanding during the period. Potential common shares consist of the incremental common shares issuable upon the exercise of options and warrants, restricted shares and convertible preferred stock. The dilutive effect of outstanding restricted shares, options and warrants is reflected in diluted earnings per share by application of the treasury stock method. Convertible preferred stock is reflected on an if-converted basis.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The following potential common shares were excluded from the computation of net loss per share because the effects were antidilutive:<B><I> </I></B>
</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align="center">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align="center">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align="center"><font face="Times New Roman">F-10</font></P>
<HR color=#C0C0C0 noShade>
<P style="page-break-before:always; margin:0pt"><P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>

<font face="Times New Roman"><B>MEDISTEM LABORATORIES, INC.</B></font></P>

<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Income Taxes</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company has adopted the provisions of SFAS No. 109, <I>
&quot;Accounting for Income Taxes&quot;</I> which requires recognition of deferred tax liabilities and assets for the expected future tax consequences of events that have been included in the consolidated financial statements or tax returns. &nbsp;Under this method, deferred tax liabilities and assets are determined based on the difference between the financial statement and tax basis of assets and liabilities using enacted tax rates in effect for the year in which the differences are expected to reverse. &nbsp;As the Company is in a significant net operating loss position, a valuation allowance has been created for all deferred tax assets as of December 31, 2007.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Loss Per Common Share</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Loss per common share is computed based on the weighted average number of common shares outstanding during each period. &nbsp;The effects of dilutive securities are not considered in the calculation of net loss per share, as their inclusion would be antidilutive.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Stock- Based Compensation</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company accounts for stock-based compensation issued to employees and non-employees as required by SFAS No. 123(R)
&quot;Accounting for Stock Based Compensation&quot; (&quot;SFAS No. 123(R)&quot;). Under these provisions, the company records expense based on the fair value of the awards utilizing the Black-Scholes pricing model for options and warrants.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Research and Development</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Expenditures for research and development are expensed as incurred. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Reclassifications</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Certain prior period amounts have been reclassified to conform to current presentation.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Royalties Receivable</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">Royalties receivable represents amounts due from the Company's licensee, ICM. &nbsp;Under the terms of the license agreement, Medistem is responsible for all transaction related expenses, including withholding taxes. &nbsp;The outstanding receivable is presented net of estimated withholding taxes (equal to 15 percent of the total royalties due). &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Functional Currency</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>&nbsp;</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company's licensee, ICM, which was consolidated until December 31, 2007, conducts most of its business activities in United States dollars. &nbsp;Accordingly, there are no translation gains or losses.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align="center"><font face="Times New Roman">F-11</font></P>
<HR color=#C0C0C0 noShade>
<P style="page-break-before:always; margin:0pt"><P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>
<font face="Times New Roman"><B>MEDISTEM LABORATORIES, INC.</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center><font face="Times New Roman"><B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B><I>Recent Accounting Pronouncements</I></B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">In July 2006, the FASB issued FASB Interpretation No. 48, Accounting for Uncertainty in Income Taxes (&quot;FIN 48&quot;)
- an interpretation of FASB Statement No. 109, Accounting for Income Taxes (&quot;SFAS No. 109&quot;). FIN 48 clarifies the accounting for uncertainty in income taxes recognized in accordance with SFAS No. 109 and prescribes a recognition threshold and measurement attribute for the financial statement recognition and measurement of a tax position taken or expected to be taken in a return. Guidance is also provided on de-recognition, classification, interest and penalties, accounting in interim periods, disclosure and transition. FIN 48 is effective for fiscal years beginning after December 15, 2006. &nbsp;The Company adopted the provisions of FIN 48 in 2007 and no material uncertain tax positions were identified. &nbsp;Thus, the adoption of FIN 48 did not have an impact on the Company's financial statements.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">In September 2006, the FASB issued SFAS No. 157, Fair Value Measurements (&quot;SFAS No. 157&quot;). SFAS No. 157 establishes a framework for measuring fair value under generally accepted accounting procedures and expands disclosures on fair value measurements. This statement applies under previously established valuation pronouncements and does not require the changing of any fair value measurements, though it may cause some valuation procedures to change. Under SFAS No. 157, fair value is established by the price that would be received to sell the item or the amount to be paid to transfer the liability of the asset as opposed to the price to be paid for the asset or received to transfer the liability. Further, it defines fair value as a market specific valuation as opposed to an entity specific valuation, though the statement does recognize that there may be instances when the low amount of market activity for a particular item or liability may challenge an entity's ability to establish a market amount. In the instances that the item is restricted, this pronouncement states that the owner of the asset or liability should take into consideration what affects the restriction would have if viewed from the perspective of the buyer or assumer of the liability. This statement is effective for all assets valued in financial statements for fiscal years beginning after November 15, 2007. The Company is currently evaluating the impact of SFAS No. 157 on its financial position and result of operations.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">In February 2007, the FASB issued SFAS No. 159, The Fair Value Option for Financial Assets and Financial Liabilities
(&quot;SFAS No. 159&quot;), which provides companies with an option to report selected financial assets and liabilities at fair value. &nbsp;SFAS No. 159 also establishes presentation and disclosure requirements designed to facilitate comparisons between companies that choose different measurement attributes for similar types of assets and liabilities. &nbsp;SFAS No. 159 is effective as of the beginning of an entity's first fiscal year beginning after November 15, 2007 with early adoption allowed. &nbsp;The Company has not yet determined the impact, if any, that adopting this standard might have on its financial statements.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">In December 2007, the FASB issued SFAS No. 141 (revised 2007), Business Combinations (&quot;SFAS No. 141(R)&quot;) and No. 160, Noncontrolling Interests in Consolidated Financial Statements, an amendment of ARB No. 51 (&quot;SFAS No. 160&quot;). SFAS No. 141(R) and SFAS No. 160 are products of a joint project between the FASB and the International Accounting Standards Board. &nbsp;The revised standards continue the movement toward the greater use of fair values in financial reporting. SFAS No. 141(R) will significantly change how business acquisitions are accounted for and will impact financial statements both on the acquisition date and in subsequent periods. These changes include the expensing of acquisition related costs and restructuring costs when incurred, the recognition of all assets, liabilities and noncontrolling interests at fair value during a step-acquisition, and the recognition of contingent consideration as of the acquisition date if it is more likely than not to be incurred. &nbsp;SFAS No. 160 will change the accounting and reporting for minority interests, which will be recharacterized as noncontrolling interests and classified as a component of equity. &nbsp;SFAS No. 141(R) and SFAS No. 160 are effective for both public and private companies for fiscal years beginning on or after December 15, 2008 (January 1, 2009 for companies with calendar year-ends). SFAS No. 141(R) will be applied prospectively. SFAS No. 160 requires retroactive adoption of the presentation and disclosure requirements for existing minority interests. All other requirements of SFAS No. 160 shall be applied prospectively. Early adoption is prohibited for both standards. &nbsp;The Company is currently evaluating the effects of these pronouncements on its financial position and results of operations.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><B>Note 6: &nbsp;Stockholders' Equity </B>
</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">On February 10, 2006, the Company authorized 200,000,000 shares of Series A Convertible Preferred Stock, par value $0.0001 per share, and amended its articles of incorporation accordingly. &nbsp;These shares are convertible into one share of common stock, have no stated interest rate, no dividend preference and liquidation preference of $0.35 per share.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">During 2006, the Company received gross proceeds totaling $1,800,000 in exchange for: (i) 5,142,858 shares of Series A Convertible Preferred Stock with a stated value of $0.35; (ii) 5,142,858 Class A Common Stock Purchase Warrants exercisable for common stock for a period of five (5) years from the date of the transaction at a per share exercise price of $0.50; and (iii) 5,142,858 Class B Common Stock Purchase Warrants exercisable for common stock for a period of five (5) years from the date of the transaction at per share exercise price of $0.75. The Company also granted an aggregate of 5,142,858 Unit Purchase Warrants (entitling the holder thereof to purchase for $0.35 one Unit comprised of one Series A Convertible Preferred Stock, one Class A Common Stock Purchase Warrant and one Class B Common Stock Purchase Warrant) of which
all such Unit Purchase warrants have expired as of December 31, 2007. &nbsp;In connection with these transactions, the Company incurred offering costs of $304,006 which were reflected as a reduction of stockholders' equity in the accompanying balance sheet.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<p align="center" style="margin: 0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">F-14</font></p>
<HR color=#C0C0C0 noShade>
<P style="page-break-before:always; margin:0pt"><P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>
<font face="Times New Roman"><B>MEDISTEM LABORATORIES, INC.</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center><font face="Times New Roman"><B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">In connection with these transactions, the Company allocated the proceeds to each instrument based on their respective fair values. &nbsp;The Company then computed the effective conversion price of each instrument, noting that the convertible preferred stock gave rise to a beneficial conversion feature in accordance with the provisions of EITF 98-5, <I>Accounting for Convertible Securities with Beneficial Conversion Features or Contingently Adjustable Conversion Ratios</I> and 00-27, <I>Application of Issue No. 98-5 to Certain Convertible Instruments</I>. &nbsp;This beneficial conversion feature was limited to $489,953 which is the amount of proceeds allocated to the convertible preferred stock. &nbsp;The entire amount associated with the beneficial conversion feature have been recognized as a deemed dividend in the year ended December 31, 2006.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">The Company granted registration rights for the Series A Convertible Preferred Stock, Class A Common Stock Purchase Warrants and Class B Common Stock Purchase Warrants as described in Note 11. In accordance with the provisions of EITF 00-19, <I>Accounting for Derivative Financial Instruments Indexed to, and Potentially Settled in, a Company's Own Stock</I>,and EITF 05-04, <I>The Effect of a Liquidated Damages Clause on a Freestanding Financial Instrument Subject to EITF Issue No. 00-19, </I>(under which it elected to consider the warrants and registration rights on a combined basis and analyzed under EITF 00-19 consistent with view A of EITF 05-04), the Company determined that these securities met the criteria for classification as stockholders' equity in the accompanying consolidated balance sheet. &nbsp;During 2006, the Company received a waiver of $50,440 of registration rights penalties owed to one investor in exchange for an extension of 3,142,858 Unit Purchase Warrants through May 2007. &nbsp;No other consideration was received. &nbsp;In connection with this waiver, the Company reclassified this amount from other current liabilities to paid-in capital in the accompanying balance sheet.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">During 2006, as part of a private placement, the Company issued an aggregate of 2,087,091 shares of common stock in exchange for cash totaling $654,500. &nbsp;All shares were issued at between $0.25 and $0.35 per share. &nbsp;In connection with these transactions, the Company incurred offering costs of $76,635 which were reflected as a reduction of stockholders' equity in the accompanying balance sheet.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">On February 1, 2006, the Company issued 3,000,000 restricted shares of common stock as compensation to two employees of ICM. &nbsp;The Company valued these grants, which vest on February 1, 2008, at $1,296,000 (net of estimated forfeitures of 10%) based on the fair market value of the Company's common stock on the date of grant and is recognizing the expense on a straight line basis over the service period. </font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">On January 2, 2007, Medistem issued an aggregate of 725,000 restricted shares of common stock as compensation to officers, directors, employees and key consultants. &nbsp;Medistem valued these grants, which vest on January 2, 2008, at $87,000 (excluding estimated forfeitures) based on the fair market value of Medistem's common stock on the date of grant and is recognizing the expense, net of estimated forfeitures of 10%, on a straight-line basis over the service period. &nbsp;For the year ended December 31, 2007, Medistem has recognized $77,871 in stock based compensation related to these restricted shares. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">On May 9, 2007, the Company entered into a collaboration agreement with the Center for Improvement of Human Functioning International, Inc., a Kansas-based non-profit organization (the
&quot;Center&quot;). &nbsp;Under the terms of the agreement, the Center transferred to Medistem all of the Center's research findings and intellectual property rights with respect to the use of a specified source of stem cells and agreed to provide continued research efforts. &nbsp;As consideration, the Company agreed to pay cash of $100,000 over the next twelve months and issue 2 million shares of common stock. &nbsp;The Company's principal stockholder, Neil Riordan, agreed to issue the shares from his personal holdings, which has been reflected as contributed capital at a fair market value of $320,000 as an increase to additional paid-in capital in the accompanying consolidated balance sheet. &nbsp;All cash consideration has been paid as of December 31, 2007.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">The Company valued the agreement at $420,000 equal to the fair value of the consideration paid based on quoted market prices on the date of grant and allocated these amounts to the respective components of the agreement based on estimated fair values. &nbsp;Of the purchase price, $355,000 was allocated to the intellectual property, which was immediately expensed as acquired in-process research and development and $65,000 was allocated to the continued research efforts, which are being expensed as incurred on a straight-line basis over the two year period for which services will be rendered. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><B>Note 7 - Options and Warrants</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><U>Adoption of FAS 123(R)</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">Effective April
21, 2005, the Financial Accounting Standards Board (&quot;FASB&quot;) issued SFAS 123(R), which is a revision of SFAS 123. SFAS 123(R) supersedes APB 25 and amends Statement of Accounting Standards No. 95,
&quot;Statement of Cash Flows&quot;. Generally, the approach in SFAS 123(R) is similar to the approach described in SFAS 123. However, SFAS123(R) requires all share-based payments to employees, including grants of employee stock options, to be recognized in the Company's Statement of Operations based on their fair values. Pro forma disclosures will no longer be an alternative. &nbsp;The Company adopted the provisions of SFAS 123(R) in the first quarter of 2006. &nbsp;As the Company had no outstanding stock options to employees at December 31, 2005, the initial adoption of SFAS 123(R) had no impact to the Company. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><U>Stock Options Granted During 2006</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">On February 1, 2006, the Company issued an aggregate of 9,850,000 stock options to various employees, directors and consultants. &nbsp;An aggregate of 7,500,000 shares underlying the stock options granted were Incentive Stock Options as defined by the Internal Revenue Code. &nbsp;All options were issued with an exercise price of $0.50, expire in ten years (or earlier in the event of termination) and are subject to the following vesting schedule:</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:36pt; text-indent:-18pt" align=justify>
<font face="Times New Roman">&#8226;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:36pt" align=justify><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">1,500,000 vested immediately;</FONT></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:36pt; text-indent:-18pt" align=justify>
<font face="Times New Roman">&#8226;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:36pt" align=justify><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">3,850,000 vested on May 1, 2006; and</FONT></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:36pt; text-indent:-18pt" align=justify>
<font face="Times New Roman">&#8226;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:36pt" align=justify><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">1,500,000 vest annually on February 1<SUP>st</SUP>, 2007, 2008 and 2009</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">The Company had previously estimated that the aggregate fair value of such stock options totaled $2,093,380 based on the Black-Scholes option pricing model using the following estimates: &nbsp;4% risk free rate, 43% volatility, and expected lives ranging from 5 to 6.5 years. &nbsp;As the Company does not have a sufficient trading history to determine the volatility of its own stock, it had based its estimate of volatility on a representative peer. &nbsp;However, during the fourth quarter of 2006, the Company revisited its policies for determining volatility, noting that the previous estimate of volatility was not based on a sufficiently large sample of peer companies. &nbsp;The Company revised its estimate of volatility from 43% to 61% which increased the value of the awards granted on February 1, 2006 from $2,093,380 to $2,711,380. &nbsp;The Company is expensing all stock options on a straight line basis over their respective vesting periods.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">During the second half of 2006, the Company issued an aggregate of 1,082,000 stock options to various employees and consultants, of which 1,080,000 were issued with an exercise price of $0.40, 1,000 were issued with an exercise price of $0.075 and 1,000 were issued with an exercise price of $0.28. &nbsp;&nbsp;Such options expire in ten years (or earlier in the event of termination) and are subject to the following vesting schedule:</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:36pt; text-indent:-18pt" align=justify>
<font face="Times New Roman">&#8226;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:36pt" align=justify><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">2,000 vested immediately</FONT></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:36pt; text-indent:-18pt" align=justify>
<font face="Times New Roman">&#8226;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:36pt" align=justify><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">600,000 vest on July 3, 2007; and</FONT></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-12pt; padding-left:36pt; text-indent:-18pt" align=justify>
<font face="Times New Roman">&#8226;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; padding-left:36pt" align=justify><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">240,000 vest annually on July 3, 2008 and 2009</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<p align="center" style="margin: 0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">F-16</font></p>
<HR color=#C0C0C0 noShade>
<P style="page-break-before:always; margin:0pt"><P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
&nbsp;<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>
<font face="Times New Roman"><B>MEDISTEM LABORATORIES, INC.</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center><font face="Times New Roman"><B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">The aggregate fair value of such stock options totaled $280,589 based on the Black-Scholes option pricing model using the following estimates: &nbsp;5.11% risk free rate, 71% volatility, and expected lives ranging from 5 to 6.5 years. &nbsp;The Company had based its estimate of volatility on its actual trading history; however, during the fourth quarter of 2006 the Company revisited its policies for determining volatility, noting that the Company's trading history was too short to be considered a valid measure of volatility. &nbsp;The Company revised its estimate of volatility from 71% to 62% based on a representative sample of peer companies, which decreased the value of these awards from $280,589 to $264,012.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">As a result of the changes in volatility applied to the 2006 awards described above, the Company' stock-based compensation expense for 2006 increased by $482,468. &nbsp;Also during the fourth quarter of 2006, the Company changed its estimated forfeiture rate on stock options and restricted stock awards from 0% to 10%. &nbsp;This change decreased stock based compensation expense by $288,507. &nbsp;All such changes were recorded during the fourth quarter of 2006.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><U>Stock Options Granted During 2007</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">During 2007, the Company issued an aggregate of 2,654,000 options to various employees and consultants. &nbsp;Such options were issued with a strike price equal to the fair market value of the stock on the date of grant (based on quoted market prices) and expire between 5 and 10 years from the date of grant (or earlier in the event of termination). &nbsp;Of the 2,654,000 options granted, 854,000 vested on the date of grant and 600,000 vest on each of the first, second and third anniversaries of the grant date. &nbsp;The aggregate grant date fair value of such awards totaled $164,484 ($148,063 net of estimated forfeitures) based on the Black-Scholes option pricing model using the following estimates: &nbsp;expected lives between 2.5 and 5.5 years, risk free rates between 3.07% and 5%, and volatility between 44% and 52%. &nbsp;The Company is expensing all stock options on a straight line basis over their respective vesting periods.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman"><U>Warrant Activity</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">On December 8, 2005, the Company issued warrants to purchase 5,000,000 shares of common stock to a third-party in exchange for investor relations services. &nbsp;The warrants, which have an exercise price of $0.25 per share, were recorded at their estimated fair value of $2,627,423 as a charge to professional fees with an offsetting credit to additional paid-in capital. &nbsp;These warrants vested at the date of grant and expire on December 7, 2008. &nbsp;The Company valued the warrants using a Black-Scholes calculation assuming a 4% risk free rate and 43% volatility. &nbsp;As indicated earlier in this footnote, the Company revisited its volatility computations in 2006. &nbsp;However, no adjustments were made to the value of the warrants granted in 2005 as the impacts were immaterial.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">During 2006, in connection with an equity offering, the Company issued warrants to purchase an aggregate of 10,285,716 shares of common stock and issued 5,142,858 of Unit Purchase Warrants (described in Note 6). &nbsp;As of December 31, 2007, all Unit Purchase Warrants expired, and no such Unit Purchase Warrants were ever exercised. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><B>Note 8: &nbsp;License Agreements</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><U>License Agreement with ICM</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">On February 23, 2006, the Company entered into a License Agreement with Institute for Cellular Medicine (&quot;ICM&quot;), a Costa Rica corporation, an entity controlled by the Company's CEO. &nbsp;This agreement was subsequently amended on November 10, 2006 to clarify certain provisions with respect to the computation of royalties. &nbsp;Under the terms of the agreement, effective retroactively to October 12, 2005, ICM received an exclusive license for the development and commercialization within Costa Rica of any new and useful process involving infusion quality umbilical cord stem cells for use in the therapeutic treatment of various medical conditions in humans. &nbsp;Medistem retains the right to manufacture and supply post-natal and adult stem cells for ICM.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">In exchange for the rights granted under the License Agreement, Medistem received (a) 85% of the pretax income resulting from Institute for Cellular Medicine's sale of any product derived from or involving infusion quality adult stem cells, and (b) 15% of the pretax income derived from non-stem cell based related activities. In addition, Medistem retained the rights to any new or useful process, manufacture, compound or composition of matter developed by Institute for Cellular Medicine relating to infusion quality umbilical cord stem cells. The License Agreement terminated on five years from the date of the agreement.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">Effective December 31, 2007, this license agreement was modified to (i) remove the funding obligation contained in the original license agreement; (ii) change the royalty rate to 20 percent of net revenues; and (iii) extend the term of the agreement from expiring in 2010 to perpetuity. &nbsp;Concurrent with the license modification, ICM no longer met the criteria for consolidation as described in Note 1.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">At December 31, 2007, Medistem had royalties receivable due from ICM (net of estimated withholding taxes) of $191,757 and other amounts due from licensee of $695,127 which was the result of the previous funding arrangement. &nbsp;All amounts due from ICM are interest-free and are repayable out of free cash flows of ICM. &nbsp;While the lack of a stated maturing date poses some risk of noncollectibility, Medistem has not reserved any amounts for potential noncollectibility as ICM has been making consistent payments over the last nine months on outstanding amounts due.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><B>Note 9: &nbsp;Related Party Transactions</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><U>License Agreement with ICM</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">The Company's Chief Executive Officer is the principal stockholder of ICM. &nbsp;See descriptions of the transactions with ICM at Note 8.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><U>Payments to Officers and Directors</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">During 2006, the Company paid $25,000 to entities controlled by the Company's Chief Executive Officer as reimbursement for research and development expenditures, respectively. &nbsp;&nbsp;During 2007, the Company paid an aggregate of $2,700 to entities controlled by the Company's Chief Executive Officer for rental use of facilities during the fourth quarter of 2007. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">During 2007, the Company's previously consolidated entity, ICM, paid an aggregate of $21,080 to the Company's Chief Medical Officer for consulting services.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">During 2007, the Company paid an aggregate of $7,260 Rivers &amp; Moorehead PLLC, an entity controlled by the Company's Chief Financial Officer, for Sarbanes-Oxley related consulting services. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><U>Agreement with Center for Improvement of Human Functioning</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">The Company's Chief Executive Officer is an officer and board member of the Center. &nbsp;See description of the agreement entered into between the Company and the Center at Note 6. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><B>Note 10: &nbsp;Minority Interest</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">As indicated in Notes 1 and 8, the Company had entered into a license agreement with ICM that is consolidated as a
VIE for which the Company is the primary beneficiary. &nbsp;Under the terms of this agreement, the Company is entitled to a royalty equal to a percentage of ICM's pretax income. &nbsp;The remaining amount of ICM's pretax income that the Company is not entitled to is reflected as minority interest in the consolidated financial statements. &nbsp;The balance of minority interest was $27,868 at the December 31, 2007, the date at which the entity was deconsolidated.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><B>Note 11: &nbsp;Commitments and Contingencies</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><U>Settlement Agreement</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">On July 12, 2007, the Company entered into an agreement with a former vendor to settle disputes related to performance under an existing contract dated December 2005. &nbsp;Under the agreement, the Company has cancelled 3.6 million warrants that were previously issued to the vendor in exchange for the issuance of 1.55 million shares of common stock and $80,000. &nbsp;Such agreement settles all prior and existing obligations with respect to the Company's contractual arrangements. &nbsp;The Company has recognized a loss of $272,000 pertaining to this settlement based on the estimated fair values of the negotiated settlement and has included this loss in general and administrative expenses in the accompanying statements of operations. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">Medistem is from time to time involved in legal proceedings arising from the normal course of business. &nbsp;There are no pending or threatened legal proceedings as of December 31, 2007.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><U>Operating Leases</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">The Company has no noncancellable operating leases. &nbsp;Leases consist of rental agreements for the use of office space in San Diego and Phoenix on a month-to-month basis.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">Rent expense totaled $177,921 and $91,825 for the years ended December 31, 2007 and 2006, respectively, and include rent under operating leases of the Company's previously consolidated licensee, ICM. &nbsp;As of December 31, 2007, rent expense for Medistem properties is approximately $1,600 per month. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>&nbsp;</P>
<p align="center" style="margin: 0pt"><font face="Times New Roman">F-20</font></p>
<HR color=#C0C0C0 noShade>
<P style="page-break-before:always; margin:0pt"><P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
&nbsp;<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>
<font face="Times New Roman"><B>MEDISTEM LABORATORIES, INC.</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center><font face="Times New Roman"><B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><U>Registration Rights</U></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">In connection with the issuance of preferred stock and related warrants described in Note 5, the Company and the investors entered into a registration rights agreement pursuant to which the Company agreed to prepare and file a
&quot;shelf&quot; registration statement with the Securities and Exchange Commission &quot;Commission&quot; covering the resale of the preferred stock and related warrants.
</font> </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">The Company did not have its registration statement declared effective until December 2006 and was in violation of the registration rights agreement. &nbsp;The Company recorded aggregate liquidated damages of $115,706 that are included in other income (expense) in the accompanying consolidated statement of operations for the year ended December 31, 2006. &nbsp;In December 2006, the Company obtained a waiver of $50,440 of liquidated damages that were accrued in exchange for an extension of 3,142,858 Unit Purchase Warrants (described in Note 7) through May 2007. &nbsp;Accordingly, the amount of waived liquidated damages were reclassified from other current liabilities to paid-in capital in the accompanying balance sheet at December 31, 2006. &nbsp;The aggregate amount of unpaid registration rights penalties, including accrued interest, was $78,032 at December 31, 2007 and is reflected as other current liabilities in the accompanying balance sheet.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman"><B>Note 12: &nbsp;Risks and Uncertainties</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">A substantial portion of the Company's revenues are derived from activities in Costa Rica and Mexico. &nbsp;The Company's operations are subject to various political, economic, and other risks and uncertainties inherent in the countries in which the Company operates. Among other risks, the Company's operations may be subject to the risks of restrictions on transfer of funds; export duties, quotas and embargoes; domestic and international customs and tariffs; changing taxation policies; foreign exchange restrictions; and political conditions and governmental regulations.</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">During 2007, the Company's affiliate, ICM, received inquiries from the Ministry of Health of Costa Rica (the
&quot;Ministry&quot;) concerning its operations. &nbsp;Based on the results of discussions with the Ministry, ICM made certain changes to its operations, none of which had a material impact on its financial condition or results of operations. &nbsp;ICM received notification that its existing operations are in compliance with its operating permits. &nbsp;However, ICM continues to receive inquiries from government officials concerning their operations. &nbsp;There can be no assurance that any such inquiries will be resolved in a manner favorable to ICM. &nbsp;</font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>

<P style="page-break-before:always; margin:0pt"><P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;</P>
&nbsp;<P style="margin:0pt" align=center>
<font face="Times New Roman"><B>MEDISTEM LABORATORIES, INC.</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center><font face="Times New Roman"><B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS</B></font></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><font face="Times New Roman">During the year ended December 31, 2007, the Company's previously consolidated licensee, ICM, exchanged laboratory equipment with a net book value of $271,970 and cash of $45,000 for laboratory equipment and a waiver of an existing payable of $155,406. &nbsp;ICM recorded the acquired laboratory equipment at $161,564 which was equal to the value of the consideration paid, and no gain or loss was recorded on the transaction.</font></P>
